   - (E, B)

2. **Path through F -> E -> L -> M -> J:**

### Minimum Cut Set:

1. Removing "E" will disconnect the first two paths.
2. Removing "H" will disconnect the third path.

By removing these two nodes, all paths between "J" and "F" are cut.

To determine the shortest path between the two black nodes (X and N), we need to follow the edges and count the intermediate nodes.

1. **Start at Node X:**
   - Node X connects to Node K, Node G, and Node F.
   - We need to find the shortest path to Node N.

2. **Explore Paths from X:**
   - **Path via G:**
     - G -> H -> I -> P -> O -> C -> N
       - Path: X -> G -> H -> I -> P -> O -> C -> N (Length: 7)
   - **Path via F:**
     - F -> E -> B -> C -> N
       - Path: X -> F -> E -> B -> C -> N (Length: 5)
   - **Path via K:**
     - K -> E -> B -> C -> N
       - Path: X -> K -> E -> B -> C -> N (Length: 5)

3. **Compare Path Lengths:**
   - The shortest paths from X to N are either via F or via K, both having a length of 5.

4. **Path via F:**
   - Path: [X, F, E, B, C, N]

5. **Path via K:**
   - Path: [X, K, E, B, C, N]

Since both paths have the same length, we can choose either one.

**Output:**

Length: 5, Path: [X, F, E, B, C, N]
